========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, hi… I guess the main thing is my head just feels foggy lately. Like I’m not thinking as clearly as I usually do. I work as a personal care aide, and I’ve been making these little mistakes in my client notes—nothing huge, but enough that I notice it, you know? And I’ll walk into a room or start to do something and then just completely blank on what it was.

It’s not really like me. I mean, I’m usually pretty on top of that stuff, especially with work notes, because one time years ago I had a supervisor who was really particular about documentation, so I got in the habit of double-checking everything… but lately even with that, I’m missing things. That’s mostly why I came in.

I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same stuff I normally do, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m “enjoying” it the same way. Like, I’ll put on a show or mess around on my phone, and then I realize I wasn’t really paying attention.

feels worn out, and simple things like helping a client shower or carrying groceries take more out of me than they should.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of before he died, like calling more or visiting sooner, and I end up feeling like I failed him as family.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: What brings me in is that my head feels foggy, and I’m making little mistakes in my client notes and forgetting what I was about to do, which isn’t like me.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I’m not planning to hurt myself, but there have been mornings when I think it wouldn’t be the worst thing if I just didn’t wake up, and that scares me.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of that time I couldn’t sit still, like if I stopped moving the grief would catch me, so I kept finding errands, calls, and chores to do.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Spanish-speaking senior-care agency right away, like I had everything figured out and couldn’t really fail.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: For several nights I slept maybe three or four hours and still got up feeling charged, making coffee more out of habit than because I felt tired.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: At work my attention kept jumping to every phone buzz, hallway noise, or random idea in my head, and then I’d lose my place in the paperwork.\n- key: S040\n  name: Increased_Goal_Activity\n  description: Increase in goal-directed No, I don’t think there’s anything where I feel no pleasure at all. That sounds pretty absolute, and I don’t know that I’d say that.

Almost no pleasure? I don’t know… that sounds stronger than what I’d say. I mean, I still get something out of things here and there.

Like if I sit down with a movie, sometimes I’m into it for a little while, but then I’ll start thinking about laundry or whether I moved the car for street cleaning — they ticket so fast on my block, it’s ridiculous — and then I’m not really paying attention anymore. So… no, I haven’t really noticed anything where it’s almost no pleasure at all. More like I’m just scattered with it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, “normal” is kind of hard for me to pin down. Some days I’ll do the usual stuff and it seems fine, like I’m not sitting there thinking, “This is terrible,” but I don’t know if I’d call it normal-normal.

I guess there are moments where I’m interested in something, but then the day gets away from me. Like I’ll start something and then remember I need to take care of some errand, or I get thinking about work schedules and who’s covering what, and then I’m off on that instead.

And I’ll walk into a room or start to do something and then just completely blank on what it was.\n\nIt’s not really like me. I mean, I’m usually pretty on top of that stuff, especially with work notes, because one time years ago I had a supervisor who was really particular about documentation, so I got in the habit of double-checking everything… but lately even with that, I’m missing things. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this foggy feeling in your head?\n\nPatient: About a month, I think. It kind of crept up on me—like at first I thought I was just tired or distracted, because everybody has days like that, you know? But lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, kind of empty feeling in my chest.\n\nIt’s funny, because with my job you sort of have to keep moving—make breakfast, help somebody with a shower, chat about the weather or whatever—and I can do all that. But inside I feel flat, like I’m just going through it. Even simple mornings feel… heavier than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same stuff I normally do, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m “enjoying” it the same way. Like, I’ll put on a show or mess around on my phone, and then I realize I wasn’t really paying attention.\n\nBut then again, some of that might just be because after work I’m kind of drained. Personal care work can be like that—some days you’re doing a lot of little things for people and keeping track of everyone’s routines, and by the time I get home I just sort of sit there. So… yeah, I guess I haven’t really thought about it clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think of something specific. I used to like little things after work, like putting on music or watching a game, but honestly I can’t tell if I don’t enjoy them or if I’m just not paying attention.\n\nLike last week I had the TV on and I kept thinking about whether I’d forgotten to buy detergent, which is ridiculous because then I ended up buying the wrong kind anyway—my sister always says I’m picky about that stuff. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more that everything feels kind of muted, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you normally find pleasurable that you've lost interest in lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say most of my daily activities, no. I mean, I get distracted from stuff, but that feels different than having no interest.

Like with chores or cooking, it’s not like those are thrilling anyway, you know? Nobody’s excited to scrub a pan. But if something comes on TV or I’m talking to someone, I can still get into it a little. Then I might drift off thinking about something else—like whether I paid that one bill, or if my phone charger is still at work.

So… I haven’t really noticed a lack of pleasure in most things. It’s more hard for me to judge than anything.

So… I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more that everything feels kind of muted, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you normally find pleasurable that you've lost interest in lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. Some days I’ll do the usual stuff and it seems fine, like I’m not sitting there thinking, “This is terrible,” but I don’t know if I’d call it normal-normal.\n\nI guess there are moments where I’m interested in something, but then the day gets away from me. Like I’ll start something and then remember I need to take care of some errand, or I get thinking about work schedules and who’s covering what, and then I’m off on that instead.\n\nSo… maybe, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a lack of interest or pleasure in most of your daily activities over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say most of my daily activities, no. I mean, I get distracted from stuff, but that feels different than having no interest.\n\nLike with chores or cooking, it’s not like those are thrilling anyway, you know? Nobody’s excited to scrub a pan. But if something comes on TV or I’m talking to someone, I can still get into it a little. Then I might drift off thinking about something else—like whether I paid that one bill, or if my phone charger is still at work.\n\nSo… I haven’t really noticed a lack of pleasure in most things. It’s more hard for me to judge than anything.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you felt much interest or pleasure in social activities lately?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 43 / personal_care_aide / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Social stuff? I mean… I haven’t really thought about it that way. I still like talking to people when it happens, like if somebody texts me or I run into someone, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m seeking it out a ton.

Sometimes I mean to make plans and then I get sidetracked—like I’ll think, “I should call so-and-so,” and then I end up dealing with groceries or looking for something I misplaced. Last week I spent way too long trying to remember where I put a receipt, which is stupid because it wasn’t even that important.

So… I guess I still have some interest in being social. I just haven’t really been keeping track of whether it’s “much” interest or not.

I’m not really sure I’d put it as little to no pleasure. Like, if it’s something I normally enjoy, I can still get something out of it sometimes. I might watch something and laugh at part of it, or start getting interested in a conversation.

But then I’ll get pulled off into some other thought, like needing to pick up something from the store or wondering where I left my keys, and by the time I come back to it, I’m not sure if I was enjoying it or just… doing it.

So I haven’t really noticed it as “no pleasure,” exactly. It’s more that I’m not sure how consistent it’s been.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, hi… I guess the main thing is my head just feels foggy lately. Like I’m not thinking as clearly as I usually do. I work as a personal care aide, and I’ve been making these little mistakes in my client notes—nothing huge, but enough that I notice it, you know? And I’ll walk into a room or start to do something and then just completely blank on what it was.\n\nIt’s not really like me. I mean, I’m usually pretty on top of that stuff, especially with work notes, because one time years ago I had a supervisor who was really particular about documentation, so I got in the habit of double-checking everything… but lately even with that, I’m missing things. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this foggy feeling in your head?\n\nPatient: About a month, I think. It kind of crept up on me—like at first I thought I was just tired or distracted, because everybody has days like that, you know? But this low, guilty, foggy feeling has been hanging around for, yeah, probably close to a month now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sad is probably the right word. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, I still go to work and I can talk to people, do what I’m supposed to do. But lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, kind of empty feeling in my chest.\n\nIt’s funny, because with my job you sort of have to keep moving—make breakfast, help somebody with a shower, chat about the weather or whatever—and I can do all that. But inside I feel flat, like I’m just going through it. Even simple mornings feel… heavier than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same stuff I normally do, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m “enjoying” it the same way. Like, I’ll put on a show or mess around on my phone, and then I realize I wasn’t really paying attention.\n\nBut then again, some of that might just be because after work I’m kind of drained. Personal care work can be like that—some days you’re doing a lot of little things for people and keeping track of everyone’s routines, and by the time I get home I just sort of sit there. So… yeah, I guess I haven’t really thought about it clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think of something specific. I used to like little things after work, like putting on music or watching a game, but honestly I can’t tell if I don’t enjoy them or if I’m just not paying attention.\n\nLike last week I had the TV on and I kept thinking about whether I’d forgotten to buy detergent, which is ridiculous because then I ended up buying the wrong kind anyway—my sister always says I’m picky about that stuff. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more that everything feels kind of muted, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you normally find pleasurable that you've lost interest in lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. It’s more like I’ll start something I normally like and then just kind of drift off. Like I’ll put on a show, and ten minutes later I’m looking up whether the sink filter needs replacing or thinking about something from work.\n\nSo… I guess maybe things aren’t grabbing me the same way, but I don’t have one clear hobby or anything where I can say, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s kind of hard for me to pin down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been tracking it, honestly. My pants fit about the same, I think… though sometimes work clothes feel weird after a long shift, but that’s probably just me being annoyed with the waistband.\n\nI don’t have a scale I check regularly. I mean, I have one somewhere, I think under the bathroom cabinet, but there’s also cleaning stuff in there and I keep meaning to organize it. So… no, I haven’t noticed any big change, at least not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but now feel no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s anything where I feel no pleasure at all. That sounds pretty absolute, and I don’t know that I’d say that.\n\nLike, if I hear a song I like, I might still like it for a minute, but then I start thinking about whether I left my work badge in the car or whether I need to call my cousin back—he always calls at the worst times—and then I’m not really in it anymore. So it’s not like the pleasure is gone completely. It’s more like I’m not staying with it long enough to tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you normally find pleasurable where you now feel almost no pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: Almost no pleasure? I don’t know… that sounds stronger than what I’d say. I mean, I still get something out of things here and there.\n\nLike if I sit down with a movie, sometimes I’m into it for a little while, but then I’ll start thinking about laundry or whether I moved the car for street cleaning — they ticket so fast on my block, it’s ridiculous — and then I’m not really paying attention anymore. But it’s not like I feel nothing from it.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed anything where it’s almost no pleasure at all. More like I’m just scattered with it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any days in the past month where you felt a normal level of pleasure or interest in your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, “normal” is kind of hard for me to pin down. Some days I’ll do the usual stuff and it seems fine, like I’m not sitting there thinking, “This is terrible,” but I don’t know if I’d call it normal-normal.\n\nI guess there are moments where I’m interested in something, but then the day gets away from me. That sounds pretty absolute, and I don’t know that I’d say that.\n\nLike, if I hear a song I like, I might still like it for a minute, but then I start thinking about whether I left my work badge in the car or whether I need to call my cousin back—he always calls at the worst times—and then I’m not really in it anymore. So it’s not like the pleasure is gone completely. I used to like little things after work, like putting on music or watching a game, but honestly I can’t tell if I don’t enjoy them or if I’m just not paying attention.\n\nLike last week I had the TV on and I kept thinking about whether I’d forgotten to buy detergent, which is ridiculous because then I ended up buying the wrong kind anyway—my sister always says I’m picky about that stuff. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more that everything feels kind of muted, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you normally find pleasurable that you've lost interest in lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. It’s more like I’ll start something I normally like and then just kind of drift off. Like I’ll put on a show, and ten minutes later I’m looking up whether the sink filter needs replacing or thinking about something from work.\n\nSo… I guess maybe things aren’t grabbing me the same way, but I don’t have one clear hobby or anything where I can say, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s kind of hard for me to pin down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been tracking it, honestly. My pants fit about the same, I think… though sometimes work clothes feel weird after a long shift, but that’s probably just me being annoyed with the waistband.\n\nI don’t have a scale I check regularly. I mean, I have one somewhere, I think under the bathroom cabinet, but there’s also cleaning stuff in there and I keep meaning to organize it. So… no, I haven’t noticed any big change, at least not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but now feel no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s anything where I feel no pleasure at all. That sounds pretty absolute, and I don’t know that I’d say that.\n\nLike, if I hear a song I like, I might still like it for a minute, but then I start thinking about whether I left my work badge in the car or whether I need to call my cousin back—he always calls at the worst times—and then I’m not really in it anymore. So it’s not like the pleasure is gone completely. It’s more like I’m not staying with it long enough to tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you normally find pleasurable where you now feel almost no pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: Almost no pleasure? I don’t know… that sounds stronger than what I’d say. But it’s not like I feel nothing from it.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed anything where it’s almost no pleasure at all. More like I’m just scattered with it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any days in the past month where you felt a normal level of pleasure or interest in your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, “normal” is kind of hard for me to pin down. Some days I’ll do the usual stuff and it seems fine, like I’m not sitting there thinking, “This is terrible,” but I don’t know if I’d call it normal-normal.\n\nI guess there are moments where I’m interested in something, but then the day gets away from me. Like I’ll start something and then remember I need to take care of some errand, or I get thinking about work schedules and who’s covering what, and then I’m off on that instead.\n\nSo… maybe, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a lack of interest or pleasure in most of your daily activities over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say most of my daily activities, no. I mean, I’m usually pretty on top of that stuff, especially with work notes, because one time years ago I had a supervisor who was really particular about documentation, so I got in the habit of double-checking everything… but lately even with that, I’m missing things. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this foggy feeling in your head?\n\nPatient: About a month, I think. It kind of crept up on me—like at first I thought I was just tired or distracted, because everybody has days like that, you know? But this low, guilty, foggy feeling has been hanging around for, yeah, probably close to a month now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sad is probably the right word. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, I still go to work and I can talk to people, do what I’m supposed to do. But lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, kind of empty feeling in my chest.\n\nIt’s funny, because with my job you sort of have to keep moving—make breakfast, help somebody with a shower, chat about the weather or whatever—and I can do all that. But inside I feel flat, like I’m just going through it. Even simple mornings feel… heavier than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same stuff I normally do, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m “enjoying” it the same way. Like, I’ll put on a show or mess around on my phone, and then I realize I wasn’t really paying attention.\n\nBut then again, some of that might just be because after work I’m kind of drained. Personal care work can be like that—some days you’re doing a lot of little things for people and keeping track of everyone’s routines, and by the time I get home I just sort of sit there. So… yeah, I guess I haven’t really thought about it clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think of something specific. I used to like little things after work, like putting on music or watching a game, but honestly I can’t tell if I don’t enjoy them or if I’m just not paying attention.\n\nLike last week I had the TV on and I kept thinking about whether I’d forgotten to buy detergent, which is ridiculous because then I ended up buying the wrong kind anyway—my sister always says I’m picky about that stuff. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more that everything feels kind of muted, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you normally find pleasurable that you've lost interest in lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. It’s more like I’ll start something I normally like and then just kind of drift off. Like I’ll put on a show, and ten minutes later I’m looking up whether the sink filter needs replacing or thinking about something from work.\n\nSo… I guess maybe things aren’t grabbing me the same way, but I don’t have one clear hobby or anything where I can say, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s kind of hard for me to pin down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been tracking it, honestly. My pants fit about the same, I think… though sometimes work clothes feel weird after a long shift, but that’s probably just me being annoyed with the waistband.\n\nI don’t have a scale I check regularly. I mean, I have one somewhere, I think under the bathroom cabinet, but there’s also cleaning stuff in there and I keep meaning to organize it. So… no, I haven’t noticed any big change, at least not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but now feel no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s anything where I feel no pleasure at all. That sounds pretty absolute, and I don’t know that I’d say that.\n\nLike, if I hear a song I like, I might still like it for a minute, but then I start thinking about whether I left my work badge in the car or whether I need to call my cousin back—he always calls at the worst times—and then I’m not really in it anymore. So it’s not like the pleasure is gone completely. It’s more like I’m not staying with it long enough to tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you normally find pleasurable where you now feel almost no pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: Almost no pleasure? I don’t know… that sounds stronger than what I’d say. I mean, I still get something out of things here and there.\n\nLike if I sit down with a movie, sometimes I’m into it for a little while, but then I’ll start thinking about laundry or whether I moved the car for street cleaning — they ticket so fast on my block, it’s ridiculous — and then I’m not really paying attention anymore. But it’s not like I feel nothing from it.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed anything where it’s almost no pleasure at all. More like I’m just scattered with it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any days in the past month where you felt a normal level of pleasure or interest in your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, “normal” is kind of hard for me to pin down. Some days I’ll do the usual stuff and it seems fine, like I’m not sitting there thinking, “This is terrible,” but I don’t know if I’d call it normal-normal.\n\nI guess there are moments where I’m interested in something, but then the day gets away from me. Like I’ll start something and then remember I need to take care of some errand, or I get thinking about work schedules and who’s covering what, and then I’m off on that instead.\n\nSo… maybe, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a lack of interest or pleasure in most of your daily activities over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say most of my daily activities, no. I mean, I get distracted from stuff, but that feels different than having no interest.\n\nLike with chores or cooking, it’s not like those are thrilling anyway, you know? Nobody’s excited to scrub a pan. But if something comes on TV or I’m talking to someone, I can still get into it a little. Then I might drift off thinking about something else—like whether I paid that one bill, or if my phone charger is still at work.\n\nSo… I haven’t really noticed a lack of pleasure in most things. It’s more hard for me to judge than anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt much interest or pleasure in social activities lately?\n\nPatient: Social stuff? I mean… I haven’t really thought about it that way. I still like talking to people when it happens, like if somebody texts me or I run into someone, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m seeking it out a ton.\n\nSometimes I mean to make plans and then I get sidetracked—like I’ll think, “I should call so-and-so,” and then I end up dealing with groceries or looking for something I misplaced. Last week I spent way too long trying to remember where I put a receipt, which is stupid because it wasn’t even that important.\n\nSo… I guess I still have some interest in being social. I just haven’t really been keeping track of whether it’s “much” interest or not.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt little to no pleasure in activities that you would normally enjoy over the past month?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I’d put it as little to no pleasure. Like, if it’s something I normally enjoy, I can still get something out of it sometimes. I might watch something and laugh at part of it, or start getting interested in a conversation.\n\nBut then I’ll get pulled off into some other thought, like needing to pick up something from the store or wondering where I left my keys, and by the time I come back to it, I’m not sure if I was enjoying it or just… doing it.\n\nSo I haven’t really noticed it as “no pleasure,” exactly. It’s more that I’m not sure how consistent it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I couldn’t give you a number or anything.\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different, I guess. Although sometimes pants fit weird depending on whether they came out of the dryer—my work scrubs especially, those things shrink in the strangest places. And then I’ll think I lost weight, but really it’s just the waistband folding funny.\n\nSo… no significant weight loss that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit over the past month?\n\nPatient: Not really, not that I’ve clearly noticed. Like I said, some clothes fit weird depending on laundry stuff—my scrubs especially, they’ll feel tighter one day and then normal the next, and I never know if that’s me or just the dryer.\n\nI did have one pair of jeans that felt kind of loose, but then I realized the belt was on a different notch than usual, and I’d been rushing that morning… so I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a definite change in how my clothes fit over the past month.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least 2 weeks recently where you felt consistently depressed or down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like I’m crying all day or can’t function, because I still get up and go to my shift and talk to people, do what I’m supposed to do.\n\nBut lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s weird, because I’ll be making breakfast or getting ready and I’ll start thinking about something random, like whether I need gas before work, and then I realize I’ve just been standing there feeling kind of sad and flat underneath it all.\n\nSo… yes, it’s been there pretty consistently. Like I can get through things, but inside I feel down.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a lack of interest or pleasure in activities that you normally enjoy most of the time over the past month?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most of the time. I still do some things I normally like, but it’s kind of… muted sometimes, I guess.\n\nLike I’ll put on a show I usually enjoy, and then I realize I’ve been looking at my phone the whole time, but that might just be because shows have gotten so drawn out lately—everything’s ten episodes when it could’ve been four. And I still like grabbing coffee before work, though half the time the place near me gets my order wrong, so then I’m annoyed instead of enjoying it.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a big clear loss of interest in everything. Maybe less enjoyment here and there, but not something I could say has been most of the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, hi… I guess the main thing is my head just feels foggy lately. Like I’m not thinking as clearly as I usually do. And I’ll walk into a room or start to do something and then just completely blank on what it was.\n\nIt’s not really like me. I mean, I’m usually pretty on top of that stuff, especially with work notes, because one time years ago I had a supervisor who was really particular about documentation, so I got in the habit of double-checking everything… but lately even with that, I’m missing things. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this foggy feeling in your head?\n\nPatient: About a month, I think. It kind of crept up on me—like at first I thought I was just tired or distracted, because everybody has days like that, you know? But this low, guilty, foggy feeling has been hanging around for, yeah, probably close to a month now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sad is probably the right word. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, I still go to work and I can talk to people, do what I’m supposed to do. But lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, kind of empty feeling in my chest.\n\nIt’s funny, because with my job you sort of have to keep moving—make breakfast, help somebody with a shower, chat about the weather or whatever—and I can do all that. But inside I feel flat, like I’m just going through it. Even simple mornings feel… heavier than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same stuff I normally do, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m “enjoying” it the same way. Like, I’ll put on a show or mess around on my phone, and then I realize I wasn’t really paying attention.\n\nBut then again, some of that might just be because after work I’m kind of drained. Personal care work can be like that—some days you’re doing a lot of little things for people and keeping track of everyone’s routines, and by the time I get home I just sort of sit there. So… yeah, I guess I haven’t really thought about it clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think of something specific. I used to like little things after work, like putting on music or watching a game, but honestly I can’t tell if I don’t enjoy them or if I’m just not paying attention.\n\nLike last week I had the TV on and I kept thinking about whether I’d forgotten to buy detergent, which is ridiculous because then I ended up buying the wrong kind anyway—my sister always says I’m picky about that stuff. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more that everything feels kind of muted, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you normally find pleasurable that you've lost interest in lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. It’s more like I’ll start something I normally like and then just kind of drift off. Like I’ll put on a show, and ten minutes later I’m looking up whether the sink filter needs replacing or thinking about something from work.\n\nSo… I guess maybe things aren’t grabbing me the same way, but I don’t have one clear hobby or anything where I can say, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s kind of hard for me to pin down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been tracking it, honestly. My pants fit about the same, I think… though sometimes work clothes feel weird after a long shift, but that’s probably just me being annoyed with the waistband.\n\nI don’t have a scale I check regularly. I mean, I have one somewhere, I think under the bathroom cabinet, but there’s also cleaning stuff in there and I keep meaning to organize it. So… no, I haven’t noticed any big change, at least not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but now feel no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s anything where I feel no pleasure at all. That sounds pretty absolute, and I don’t know that I’d say that.\n\nLike, if I hear a song I like, I might still like it for a minute, but then I start thinking about whether I left my work badge in the car or whether I need to call my cousin back—he always calls at the worst times—and then I’m not really in it anymore. So it’s not like the pleasure is gone completely. It’s more like I’m not staying with it long enough to tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you normally find pleasurable where you now feel almost no pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: Almost no pleasure? I don’t know… that sounds stronger than what I’d say. I mean, I still get something out of things here and there.\n\nLike if I sit down with a movie, sometimes I’m into it for a little while, but then I’ll start thinking about laundry or whether I moved the car for street cleaning — they ticket so fast on my block, it’s ridiculous — and then I’m not really paying attention anymore. But it’s not like I feel nothing from it.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed anything where it’s almost no pleasure at all. More like I’m just scattered with it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any days in the past month where you felt a normal level of pleasure or interest in your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, “normal” is kind of hard for me to pin down. Some days I’ll do the usual stuff and it seems fine, like I’m not sitting there thinking, “This is terrible,” but I don’t know if I’d call it normal-normal.\n\nI guess there are moments where I’m interested in something, but then the day gets away from me. Like I’ll start something and then remember I need to take care of some errand, or I get thinking about work schedules and who’s covering what, and then I’m off on that instead.\n\nSo… maybe, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a lack of interest or pleasure in most of your daily activities over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say most of my daily activities, no. I mean, I get distracted from stuff, but that feels different than having no interest.\n\nLike with chores or cooking, it’s not like those are thrilling anyway, you know? Nobody’s excited to scrub a pan. But if something comes on TV or I’m talking to someone, I can still get into it a little. Then I might drift off thinking about something else—like whether I paid that one bill, or if my phone charger is still at work.\n\nSo… I haven’t really noticed a lack of pleasure in most things. It’s more hard for me to judge than anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt much interest or pleasure in social activities lately?\n\nPatient: Social stuff? I mean… I haven’t really thought about it that way. Last week I spent way too long trying to remember where I put a receipt, which is stupid because it wasn’t even that important.\n\nSo… I guess I still have some interest in being social. I just haven’t really been keeping track of whether it’s “much” interest or not.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt little to no pleasure in activities that you would normally enjoy over the past month?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I’d put it as little to no pleasure. Like, if it’s something I normally enjoy, I can still get something out of it sometimes. I might watch something and laugh at part of it, or start getting interested in a conversation.\n\nBut then I’ll get pulled off into some other thought, like needing to pick up something from the store or wondering where I left my keys, and by the time I come back to it, I’m not sure if I was enjoying it or just… doing it.\n\nSo I haven’t really noticed it as “no pleasure,” exactly. It’s more that I’m not sure how consistent it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I couldn’t give you a number or anything.\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different, I guess. I work as a personal care aide, and I’ve been making these little mistakes in my client notes—nothing huge, but enough that I notice it, you know? And I’ll walk into a room or start to do something and then just completely blank on what it was.\n\nIt’s not really like me. I mean, I’m usually pretty on top of that stuff, especially with work notes, because one time years ago I had a supervisor who was really particular about documentation, so I got in the habit of double-checking everything… but lately even with that, I’m missing things. That’s mostly why I came in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this foggy feeling in your head?\n\nPatient: About a month, I think. It kind of crept up on me—like at first I thought I was just tired or distracted, because everybody has days like that, you know? But this low, guilty, foggy feeling has been hanging around for, yeah, probably close to a month now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, sad is probably the right word. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, I still go to work and I can talk to people, do what I’m supposed to do. But lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, kind of empty feeling in my chest.\n\nIt’s funny, because with my job you sort of have to keep moving—make breakfast, help somebody with a shower, chat about the weather or whatever—and I can do all that. But inside I feel flat, like I’m just going through it. Even simple mornings feel… heavier than they should.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I still do some of the same stuff I normally do, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m “enjoying” it the same way. Like, I’ll put on a show or mess around on my phone, and then I realize I wasn’t really paying attention.\n\nBut then again, some of that might just be because after work I’m kind of drained. Personal care work can be like that—some days you’re doing a lot of little things for people and keeping track of everyone’s routines, and by the time I get home I just sort of sit there. So… yeah, I guess I haven’t really thought about it clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing that you no longer find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m trying to think of something specific. I used to like little things after work, like putting on music or watching a game, but honestly I can’t tell if I don’t enjoy them or if I’m just not paying attention.\n\nLike last week I had the TV on and I kept thinking about whether I’d forgotten to buy detergent, which is ridiculous because then I ended up buying the wrong kind anyway—my sister always says I’m picky about that stuff. So… I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear thing where I’m like, “I hate this now.” It’s more that everything feels kind of muted, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there things you normally find pleasurable that you've lost interest in lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. It’s more like I’ll start something I normally like and then just kind of drift off. Like I’ll put on a show, and ten minutes later I’m looking up whether the sink filter needs replacing or thinking about something from work.\n\nSo… I guess maybe things aren’t grabbing me the same way, but I don’t have one clear hobby or anything where I can say, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s kind of hard for me to pin down.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really been tracking it, honestly. My pants fit about the same, I think… though sometimes work clothes feel weird after a long shift, but that’s probably just me being annoyed with the waistband.\n\nI don’t have a scale I check regularly. I mean, I have one somewhere, I think under the bathroom cabinet, but there’s also cleaning stuff in there and I keep meaning to organize it. So… no, I haven’t noticed any big change, at least not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasurable but now feel no pleasure in at all?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think there’s anything where I feel no pleasure at all. That sounds pretty absolute, and I don’t know that I’d say that.\n\nLike, if I hear a song I like, I might still like it for a minute, but then I start thinking about whether I left my work badge in the car or whether I need to call my cousin back—he always calls at the worst times—and then I’m not really in it anymore. So it’s not like the pleasure is gone completely. It’s more like I’m not staying with it long enough to tell.\n\nDoctor: Are there activities that you normally find pleasurable where you now feel almost no pleasure at all?\n\nPatient: Almost no pleasure? I don’t know… that sounds stronger than what I’d say. I mean, I still get something out of things here and there.\n\nLike if I sit down with a movie, sometimes I’m into it for a little while, but then I’ll start thinking about laundry or whether I moved the car for street cleaning — they ticket so fast on my block, it’s ridiculous — and then I’m not really paying attention anymore. But it’s not like I feel nothing from it.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed anything where it’s almost no pleasure at all. More like I’m just scattered with it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any days in the past month where you felt a normal level of pleasure or interest in your usual activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, “normal” is kind of hard for me to pin down. Some days I’ll do the usual stuff and it seems fine, like I’m not sitting there thinking, “This is terrible,” but I don’t know if I’d call it normal-normal.\n\nI guess there are moments where I’m interested in something, but then the day gets away from me. Like I’ll start something and then remember I need to take care of some errand, or I get thinking about work schedules and who’s covering what, and then I’m off on that instead.\n\nSo… maybe, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a lack of interest or pleasure in most of your daily activities over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say most of my daily activities, no. I mean, I get distracted from stuff, but that feels different than having no interest.\n\nLike with chores or cooking, it’s not like those are thrilling anyway, you know? Nobody’s excited to scrub a pan. But if something comes on TV or I’m talking to someone, I can still get into it a little. Then I might drift off thinking about something else—like whether I paid that one bill, or if my phone charger is still at work.\n\nSo… I haven’t really noticed a lack of pleasure in most things. It’s more hard for me to judge than anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt much interest or pleasure in social activities lately?\n\nPatient: Social stuff? I mean… I haven’t really thought about it that way. I still like talking to people when it happens, like if somebody texts me or I run into someone, but I don’t know if I’d say I’m seeking it out a ton.\n\nSometimes I mean to make plans and then I get sidetracked—like I’ll think, “I should call so-and-so,” and then I end up dealing with groceries or looking for something I misplaced. Last week I spent way too long trying to remember where I put a receipt, which is stupid because it wasn’t even that important.\n\nSo… I guess I still have some interest in being social. I just haven’t really been keeping track of whether it’s “much” interest or not.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt little to no pleasure in activities that you would normally enjoy over the past month?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I’d put it as little to no pleasure. Like, if it’s something I normally enjoy, I can still get something out of it sometimes. I might watch something and laugh at part of it, or start getting interested in a conversation.\n\nBut then I’ll get pulled off into some other thought, like needing to pick up something from the store or wondering where I left my keys, and by the time I come back to it, I’m not sure if I was enjoying it or just… doing it.\n\nSo I haven’t really noticed it as “no pleasure,” exactly. It’s more that I’m not sure how consistent it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I couldn’t give you a number or anything.\n\nMy clothes don’t feel dramatically different, I guess. Although sometimes pants fit weird depending on whether they came out of the dryer—my work scrubs especially, those things shrink in the strangest places. And then I’ll think I lost weight, but really it’s just the waistband folding funny.\n\nSo… no significant weight loss that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your clothes fit over the past month?\n\nPatient: Not really, not that I’ve clearly noticed. Like I said, some clothes fit weird depending on laundry stuff—my scrubs especially, they’ll feel tighter one day and then normal the next, and I never know if that’s me or just the dryer.\n\nI did have one pair of jeans that felt kind of loose, but then I realized the belt was on a different notch than usual, and I’d been rushing that morning… so I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a definite change in how my clothes fit over the past month.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least 2 weeks recently where you felt consistently depressed or down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like I’m crying all day or can’t function, because I still get up and go to my shift and talk to people, do what I’m supposed to do.\n\nBut lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s weird, because I’ll be making breakfast or getting ready and I’ll start thinking about something random, like whether I need gas before work, and then I realize I’ve just been standing there feeling kind of sad and flat underneath it all.\n\nSo… yes, it’s been there pretty consistently. Like I can get through things, but inside I feel down.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a lack of interest or pleasure in activities that you normally enjoy most of the time over the past month?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most of the time. I still do some things I normally like, but it’s kind of… muted sometimes, I guess.\n\nLike I’ll put on a show I usually enjoy, and then I realize I’ve been looking at my phone the whole time, but that might just be because shows have gotten so drawn out lately—everything’s ten episodes when it could’ve been four. And I still like grabbing coffee before work, though half the time the place near me gets my order wrong, so then I’m annoyed instead of enjoying it.\n\nSo… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a big clear loss of interest in everything. Maybe less enjoyment here and there, but not something I could say has been most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past month where you felt completely free of depressive symptoms?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, there are moments where I’m distracted enough that I’m not thinking about it—like if work gets busy, or I’m trying to find where I put my keys, which somehow are never where I swear I left them.\n\nBut if you mean a whole day where that low, guilty, kind of foggy feeling isn’t there at all… I don’t think so. It’s been kind of hanging over me for about a month now, even when I’m doing normal stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not like I’m making a plan or anything. I’m not trying to hurt myself.\n\nBut there have been a few mornings where I wake up and just… I don’t know, I think, “If I hadn’t woken up, maybe that wouldn’t have been the worst thing.” And then I feel bad even thinking that, because I know people have real problems, and I’ve got people depending on me at work and all that.\n\nIt scares me, honestly. Like I don’t want to do anything, but the thought being there at all freaks me out.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a lack of pleasure in activities that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a lack of pleasure exactly. Like, I still do things I normally like, but sometimes I’m kind of half there, you know?\n\nI’ll start watching something or make coffee, and then I’m distracted by some random thing—like my phone, or remembering I forgot to take the trash out—and then I’m not sure if I’m not enjoying it or if I’m just not paying attention. So… maybe a little dulled sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed it as this big clear thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
